The former false row `directory_import_csv.py::col` is deleted: its outer
`import_employees_csv_bytes` writer remains W077. The former `_unexpected_hook` attribution
is corrected to the enclosing test that owns the UPDATE, while the two real nested Contact
test workers use fully qualified `<locals>` identities. Former duplicate rows W491–W498,
W500–W501 are deleted rather than aliased; later rows were renumbered and those identifiers
may now denote different unique callables. W594 supplies the previously absent guarded
`l9...::downgrade` identity.

Each ADR reference is the source line in the frozen normative blob. The first ASCII token
of every disposition cell is exactly `migrate` or `disable` and is the sole disposition;
later wording describes the required change or a future separately reviewed revision, not
an alternate disposition. A row containing
multiple explicitly named callables still requires an individual manifest record and proof
for every named terminal/caller edge; it is one W-row only because ADR §9.3 makes that exact
closed set one normative disposition. `verified non-writer` rows remain deny-on-DML gates.
No wildcard grants permission to an unknown callable. Every disposition is explicit;
`migrate or disable` in the ADR is resolved here to `disable` until a separately reviewed
migration proof enables the named path.

**Manifest artifacts.**

- new `app/personnel_lk/application/adr065_writer_manifest.json` is generated from this
  closed W001–W594 source; it records source WP/ADR
  hashes, file, callable, normalized SQL fingerprint, entities, entrypoint, disposition,
  required lock classes and proof IDs;
- new `scripts/ci/verify_adr065_writer_manifest.py` compares AST/data-flow, normalized SQL,
  route graph, runtime branch evidence and catalog writers against that reviewed source. It
  may report drift but may not add, classify or waive a writer;
- new `tests/personnel_lk/test_adr065_writer_inventory.py` asserts (a) every ADR §9.3 row
  maps to exactly one W001–W130 row, (b) repository-wide direct/indirect DML scan results
  map to W001–W594, and (c) every manifest record maps back to exactly one approved row. It
  reproduces the nested read-only `directory_import_csv.col` case and requires ownership by
  `import_employees_csv_bytes`; rejects duplicate callable identity, ambiguous disposition,
  missing/unknown writer and lexical-owner mismatch; treats migration `upgrade` and
  `downgrade` as separate required identities; and asserts all W-IDs are unique, continuous
  and complete. It pairs
  apply concurrency with boundary/C2, import, order/application, enrollment/onboarding,
  notification/ACK, reconciliation, Contact, audit and disabled maintenance paths.

The WP matrix is the sole source of truth; the generated manifest detects drift and never
adds a row, chooses a disposition or alters scope. ADR §9.3→inventory and repository
scan→inventory are independent fail-closed checks. Any unknown/dynamic writer, missing
expected row, duplicate callable identity, lexical-owner mismatch, upgrade lacking the
required downgrade record, renamed/removed callable without explicit disposition, changed
fingerprint, missing proof, unexpected catalog writer, or
manifest/hash mismatch emits `ADR065_WRITER_MANIFEST_DRIFT` and disables ADR-065 apply
and UI apply immediately. Read-only discovery remains available only if its independent
readiness checks pass.

**Ready when.** Static callsite inventory, runtime lock evidence and catalog writer evidence
all agree; every writer uses the common order or is technically disabled; direct assignment
writes outside strict C2 are removed from enabled paths; startup keeps apply disabled on
artifact/hash drift.

---

## 6. Global lock and writer protocol

All mutating slices use one shared lock helper. Unlocked discovery may collect IDs only.
After replay miss, decisions use rows reread under locks. Used classes may be skipped, but
the transaction never returns to an earlier class:

1. operation advisory lock and operation row;
2. shared boundary advisory lock, then watermark `FOR SHARE`;
3. identity advisory locks;
4. org-unit rows, then position rows, sorted, `FOR SHARE`;
5. personnel-order evidence scopes sorted by order ID `FOR UPDATE`;
6. order header, items, bases and attachments, in that order and by PK, `FOR SHARE`;
7. Person rows by `person_id` (the P0 Shell immediately after INSERT);
8. assignment-scope advisory lock by `person_id`;
9. Employee rows by `employee_id`;
10. assignments by `assignment_id`;
11. assignment links by `link_id`;
12. import batch/row/normalized records by the ADR ordering;
13. Contact rows, then later projections/audit/result rows.

The boundary worker takes the exclusive form of the boundary lock and watermark
`FOR UPDATE`. Identity serialization protects absent Person rows; Person-scope
serialization and database constraints protect absent assignments. A concurrent Person,
link or assignment appearing after preview cannot be adopted opportunistically unless the
exact ADR branch permits it; normally the rebuilt state digest returns
`STALE_EXPECTED_STATE` before business DML.

---

## 7. Stale state, retry and idempotency

### 7.1. Stale-state rules

- Preview binds full finite state, not only IDs.
- Apply validates token/context, takes all locks, rereads all participating rows and
  collections, rebuilds JCS and compares before the first business DML.
- Concurrent Person candidate appearance, Employee link, identity change, assignment,
  evidence generation, import membership, org/position rename or watermark movement is
  stale or the more specific ADR error; it never causes best-effort continuation.
- Initial malformed order scope is `ORDER_EVIDENCE_SCOPE_INVALID`; drift after a valid
  preview is `STALE_EXPECTED_STATE`.
- Only a current singleton watermark with `effective_date=D` is usable.

### 7.2. Retry rules

| Situation | Result |
|---|---|
| Same scoped idempotency key, same request, committed success | stored success, `replayed=true`, no personnel reads/writes |
| Same key, different digest | `IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_REUSED`, no mutation |
| Concurrent same key/same request | one execution; waiter returns stored result |
| Transaction rollback | operation row and all domain rows disappear; same key/request starts one fresh attempt |
| Unknown commit outcome | client retries the same key/request only |
| Serialization/deadlock | whole transaction may retry under ADR bounded policy; no partial retry |
| Domain unique/exclusion conflict | rollback and deterministic reread/mapping; never blind retry |

The generated operation ID is persisted only after the operation row exists and is used as
an FK/provenance reference. It is never part of the pre-operation request digest.

---

## 8. PPR read-path preservation

The following files are read-only compatibility guards, not implementation targets:

- `app/api/ppr_router.py`
- `app/api/ppr_errors.py`
- `app/ppr/read/query_service.py`
- `app/ppr/infrastructure/identity_repository.py`
- `corpsite-ui/app/directory/personnel/_lib/pprQueryApi.client.ts`
- `corpsite-ui/app/directory/personnel/_lib/pprCardPresentation.ts`

Before repair commit, `employees.person_id=NULL` continues to raise
`PprEmployeePersonLinkMissingError`, mapped to HTTP `409`. The repair operation does not
weaken, bypass or auto-fix this resolver. During an in-flight transaction other sessions
must still see the old committed state and receive `409`. Only after the full composite
commit may the unchanged resolver find Person and read the card. A failed/rolled-back
repair must leave the same `409` behavior.

---

## 9. Mandatory acceptance and test matrix

| ID | Scenario | Required proof |
|---|---|---|
| AC-01 | Successful composite P0 | one Shell, exact Employee link/IIN, one C2 primary, link/reconciliation, P0 events, audit, provenance/import/Contact/result; one commit |
| AC-02 | Failure after every physical write boundary | full pre-operation snapshot restored; no successful operation row/result |
| AC-03 | Concurrent Person appears | lock serialization then stale/conflict; no duplicate Person and no partial link |
| AC-04 | Concurrent Employee link appears | stale/conflict before business DML; no overwrite |
| AC-05 | Concurrent primary assignment appears | stale/unique/exclusion result; no second primary |
| AC-06 | Same key/same request after commit | stored replay; no additional rows or sequences |
| AC-07 | Same key/different request | `IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_REUSED`; no result disclosure/mutation |
| AC-08 | Retry after rollback | starts from original committed P0 state and succeeds at most once |
| AC-09 | Stale expected state | exact precedence; zero business DML |
| AC-10 | Partial unique conflict | database rejects second current active primary; rollback |
| AC-11 | Exclusion conflict | overlapping non-VOID primary rejected; rollback |
| AC-12 | Strict audit failure | entire transaction rolls back |
| AC-13 | Event/provenance/import binding failure | entire transaction rolls back |
| AC-14 | Each of eight decisions missing/unconfirmed | `ASSIGNMENT_INTENT_INCOMPLETE`; no classification/apply |
| AC-15 | Import/Employee/HIRE/order contains tempting values | no assignment input is populated or inferred |
| AC-16 | PPR during uncommitted repair | unchanged GET returns fail-closed `409` |
| AC-17 | PPR after complete commit | unchanged GET resolves the new Person/card |
| AC-18 | PPR after rollback | unchanged GET remains `409` |
| AC-19 | P1 encountered while P1 activation is disabled | remains a P1 preview; no P0/create/link-only fallback and no write |
| AC-20 | Exact/semantic C2 adoption | only ADR-compatible single owner; replay deterministic; ambiguity/conflict rolls back |
| AC-21 | Complete two-step P0 preview wire/token | prepare returns every safe confirmation member; confirm exact byte echo is recomputed; full intention/state/digests and valid 1800-second token; no DML |
| AC-22 | Preview context/key failures | actor/role/target/correlation/import/state/watermark mismatch, tamper, unknown/retired/not-yet-valid/expired key fail closed without sensitive data |
| AC-23 | Boundary claim/finalize | exact T0/T1 lifecycle, completed evidence and watermark advance occur once; runtime cannot direct-DML evidence |
| AC-24 | Boundary crash/recovery/retry | expired T0 lease recovers once; T1-incomplete commit aborts; retry lineage is single/immutable |
| AC-25 | Delivery/ACK prerequisite | missing, unclaimed, wrong-digest or unacknowledged attempt prevents boundary finalization/watermark advance |
| AC-26 | Writer-manifest drift | unknown/changed/static-only/runtime-only/catalog-only writer disables apply/UI before business DML |
| AC-27 | Immediate stop events | first unique/exclusion/audit/provenance/idempotency failure rolls back and opens readiness circuit; PPR unchanged |
| AC-28 | Computed stale/error thresholds | PostgreSQL aggregates every worker over exact 900-second/minimum-count/basis-point boundaries, disables at equality and re-enables only by generation-CAS after a clean gate |
| AC-29 | Confirmation echo alteration | missing/extra/changed echo member, copied fingerprint/verifier, state/actor/context/environment drift or stale material yields the exact malformed/mismatch/stale blocker and no token/write |
| AC-30 | Four crypto profiles | each dedicated ring signs/derives only its purpose; active/previous/retired/unknown/environment and rotation boundaries pass; cross-profile secret/key substitution always fails readiness |
| AC-31 | Multi-worker runtime stop | combined events trip once when individual workers are below threshold; no post-trip admission succeeds, healthy backend rejection is <=3s and UI reflection <=5s, restart preserves trip, storage loss fails closed, and a durable pre-trip admission finishes atomically without blocking trip |

Test layers are mandatory:

- pure/schema/JCS vectors;
- authority-port unit tests with caller-owned connections;
- PostgreSQL migration/catalog/constraint tests;
- PostgreSQL role/grant, SECURITY DEFINER function, boundary lifecycle and delivery/ACK
  tests;
- real FastAPI integration tests;
- concurrency tests with separate connections and controlled lock barriers;
- statement-ordinal fault injection and complete snapshots;
- startup/readiness, key-ring rotation, manifest drift, metric-window and stop-circuit tests;
- frontend client/component/integration tests;
- production-like read-only acceptance before activation, followed by separately authorized
  operator verification—never ad hoc SQL repair.

---

## 10. Implementation sequence

1. Approve this WP against the frozen baselines.
2. Implement Slice 1 expected-state/token only; keep `apply_available=false`.
3. Create and review the exact Slice 2 `m0→n1→o2` migrations on the clean
   `l9m0n1o2p3q4` head; run data/catalog/role preflight without repair.
4. Implement Slice 3 idempotency/locked-state protocol with no domain dispatcher.
5. Implement and independently verify Slice 4 authority ports.
6. Implement Slice 5 atomic service and exhaustive rollback/concurrency tests; keep it
   unreachable from HTTP.
7. Add Slice 6 operator API behind the common disabled rollout gate.
8. Implement the closed Slice 8 W001–W594 dispositions; verify manifest drift,
   boundary/delivery runtime, preview/evidence keys, watermark and all stop signals.
9. Enable P0 API for a controlled non-production/test cohort; run backend re-review.
10. Implement Slice 7 UI and enable the button only after backend readiness is proven.
11. Perform authorized acceptance of the three production scenario IDs through the UI;
    do not run manual SQL repair.

---

## 11. Migration, rollout and rollback plan

### 11.1. Migration

1. Start from tracked `l9m0n1o2p3q4`; exclude the unrelated untracked termination
   revision and verify reserved IDs `m0`, `n1`, `o2` are unique.
2. Run assignment/provenance/event, boundary-role/session and delivery/onboarding legacy
   preflights; any finding blocks before DDL and performs no repair.
3. Apply the closed boundary chain `m0n1o2p3q4r5`, then `n1o2p3q4r5s6`, then
   `o2p3q4r5s6t7`; apply rollout-control `p3q4r5s6t7u8` only after their catalog and
   lifecycle proofs pass.
4. Install hard guarantees only after deterministic validation/backfill explicitly allowed
   by ADR; no business repair is performed by migration.
5. Verify exact catalog, FK actions, triggers/functions, predicates, roles/grants and the
   single reviewed Alembic head.

### 11.2. Rollout

1. Deploy schema with apply disabled.
2. Deploy common lock helpers; migrate or technically disable all W001–W594 entries and prove
   exact deployed manifest/hash.
3. Deploy boundary/delivery runtime roles and C2 runner dark; recover/finalize no retained
   incomplete run, then complete one valid catch-up with delivery/ACK/audit evidence.
4. Verify preview and evidence key rings/profiles/rotation, retained keys and no secret
   exposure.
5. Verify exactly one current watermark at independently computed `D`, matching completed
   boundary evidence, and healthy C2 advancement.
6. Deploy backend apply dark; run migration, port, concurrency, fault-injection,
   observability and stop-circuit gates.
7. Complete read-only backend review.
8. Explicitly set `ADR065_APPLY_ENABLED=true` for the controlled P0 API cohort.
9. Deploy and enable UI only after backend acceptance and a green readiness response.

### 11.3. Exact readiness, observability and stop protocol

**One shared stop-state implementation.** PostgreSQL is the sole global runtime circuit;
the existing deployment has one Uvicorn process today, but correctness never depends on
worker count or process memory. Add migration
`alembic/versions/p3q4r5s6t7u8_adr065_runtime_stop_state.py` with
`revision=p3q4r5s6t7u8` and `down_revision=o2p3q4r5s6t7`, plus
`app/personnel_lk/infrastructure/adr065_runtime_stop_repository.py`,
`app/personnel_lk/application/adr065_runtime_stop_service.py` and
`app/personnel_lk/application/adr065_observability.py`. Extend
`adr065_readiness_service.py`, `app/config.py`, `app/main.py`, the Slice-6 routes and the
single Slice-7 UI client/component. This new rollout-control migration follows, and does
not alter, the already closed `m0→n1→o2` boundary protocol.

The migration creates:

- singleton `adr065_runtime_stop_state(singleton boolean PRIMARY KEY CHECK(singleton),
  state text CHECK IN ('ENABLED','TRIPPED','RECOVERING'), generation bigint positive,
  reason_code text NULL, tripped_at timestamptz NULL, recovery_started_at timestamptz NULL,
  recovered_at timestamptz NULL, readiness_digest char(64) NULL, updated_at timestamptz,
  transition_actor_user_id bigint NULL)` with state/NULL-shape CHECKs;
- append-only `adr065_runtime_stop_events(event_id bigint identity PRIMARY KEY,
  occurred_at timestamptz, worker_instance_id uuid, request_correlation_fingerprint
  char(64), outcome_class text CHECK IN ('SUCCESS','EXPECTED_REJECTION','STALE',
  'UNEXPECTED_ERROR','IMMEDIATE_STOP'), reason_code text NULL, observed_generation bigint)`
  and index `(occurred_at,event_id)`; values are safe, never IIN/request payload;
- append-only `adr065_runtime_stop_transitions(transition_id bigint identity PRIMARY KEY,
  occurred_at timestamptz, from_state, to_state, prior_generation, new_generation,
  reason_code, window_total, window_matched, actor_user_id, readiness_digest)` as immutable
  audit/recovery evidence;
- `adr065_runtime_admissions(admission_id uuid PRIMARY KEY, admitted_generation bigint,
  worker_instance_id uuid, request_correlation_fingerprint char(64), state text CHECK IN
  ('ADMITTED','SUCCEEDED','EXPECTED_REJECTION','STALE','UNEXPECTED_ERROR','UNKNOWN_COMMIT'),
  admitted_at timestamptz, heartbeat_at timestamptz, lease_expires_at timestamptz,
  terminalized_at timestamptz NULL, outcome_class text NULL, reason_code text NULL)` with
  FK-like generation validation in SECURITY-DEFINER functions, exact state/null-shape
  CHECKs, index `(state,lease_expires_at,admission_id)`, and no raw key, IIN or request.

The singleton shape is exact: ENABLED has null reason/tripped/recovery-start values (and
may retain the last `recovered_at`); TRIPPED has non-null reason and `tripped_at` with null
recovery start; RECOVERING retains that trip evidence and has non-null
`recovery_started_at`. Every successful trip, begin-recovery and complete-recovery CAS sets
`generation=prior_generation+1` and `updated_at=transaction_timestamp()`; automatic trips
have null actor, controlled recovery requires a non-null actor.

NOLOGIN role `adr065_runtime_control_owner` owns the four tables and functions. Role
`adr065_apply_runtime` receives SELECT on the singleton and EXECUTE only on
admit/heartbeat/finalize/record/trip;
role `adr065_rollout_recovery` is a separate NOLOGIN grant target for the controlled
operator workload and receives only recovery/cleanup EXECUTE. PUBLIC is revoked from all
objects. Neither runtime role receives direct table DML. Functions use fixed
`SET search_path=pg_catalog,public`, qualify every object, validate the closed reason-code
set in the readiness table below and are the only mutation paths:

- `admit_adr065_apply(...)` runs in its own short control transaction before the
  caller-owned business transaction exists. It locks the singleton row `FOR UPDATE`,
  rejects non-`ENABLED`, expired `ADMITTED` leases and every already-met rolling/binary
  stop condition, atomically trips when required, or inserts one `ADMITTED` row bound to
  the current generation and commits. The returned `admission_id,generation,lease_expires_at`
  are operational authority only and never become business request inputs;
- `heartbeat_adr065_admission(admission_id,generation,worker)` extends the lease to
  `transaction_timestamp()+5s` only for the same live `ADMITTED` generation. The worker
  invokes it every second on a separate control connection; it never touches the business
  transaction;
- `finalize_adr065_admission(...)` conditionally terminalizes one admission and inserts its
  global outcome event in the same short control transaction. Under the singleton
  `FOR UPDATE` lock it aggregates all workers over `[transaction_timestamp()-900s,
  transaction_timestamp()]` and performs the single CAS `ENABLED→TRIPPED` plus one
  transition when stale has at least 20 requests and `STALE/total >=25%`, or unexpected
  error has at least 10 attempts and `UNEXPECTED_ERROR/total >=10%`;
- `sweep_expired_adr065_admissions()` runs once per second and is also invoked at the start
  of every admission. Any expired `ADMITTED` row is atomically marked `UNKNOWN_COMMIT` and
  trips with `ADR065_ADMISSION_LEASE_EXPIRED`; a worker crash therefore cannot reopen or
  silently lose a terminal outcome;
- `record_adr065_runtime_outcome(...)` remains only for non-apply binary readiness signals,
  uses the same short singleton serialization and cannot create an admission;
- `trip_adr065_runtime_stop(...)` performs the same atomic trip for any binary immediate
  stop signal in the table below. Concurrent admission/trip calls serialize only on the
  short singleton row lock; no business transaction holds it;
- `begin_adr065_runtime_recovery(expected_generation,actor)` performs
  `TRIPPED→RECOVERING`; `complete_adr065_runtime_recovery(expected_generation,
  readiness_digest,actor)` performs `RECOVERING→ENABLED` only after the full external
  readiness suite supplies the matching digest, every admission is terminal, no
  `UNKNOWN_COMMIT` remains unresolved and one full configured metric window is clean.
  Both are generation-CAS operations; a stale worker cannot recover or enable;
- `cleanup_adr065_runtime_stop_events(cutoff)` removes only terminal events and admissions
  older than 30 days, never nonterminal admissions, through the recovery role and records
  cleanup evidence; transition evidence is retained indefinitely. Direct/manual SQL
  switching or repair is forbidden.

The chosen protocol is **short durable admission, never a business-transaction-held gate
lock**. Each request first checks static `ADR065_APPLY_ENABLED`, then synchronously calls
`admit_adr065_apply` and commits that short control transaction. Only a successful durable
admission permits creation of the one caller-owned ADR-065 business transaction. The
business transaction never locks the stop singleton or an advisory stop key. Threshold
recording/trip therefore never waits for Person/Employee/C2/audit/provenance work.

The singleton row is the admission serialization point. If admission obtains its row lock
first and commits `ADMITTED`, a concurrent later trip may commit while that operation is
running; this operation was admitted before the stop and may finish its atomic business
transaction. If trip obtains the row first, it commits `TRIPPED` and admission rejects.
After successful admission no pre-commit stop recheck is performed: ADR atomicity and the
no-compensation rule require the already-admitted operation either to commit fully or roll
back for its own business failure. It can never partially commit a link. The heartbeat is
operational evidence only and cannot cancel or authorize domain DML.

After business commit/rollback the service must synchronously terminalize the durable
admission before returning. Unknown business commit is recorded as `UNKNOWN_COMMIT` and
trips; a terminalization/control error leaves an expiring admission, stops that worker
locally and is converted by the one-second sweeper or next admission into the same global
trip. Storage/read/commit-ack unavailability returns
`ADR065_RUNTIME_STOP_STATE_UNAVAILABLE`; no new business transaction opens. Worker restart
retains admission/trip evidence, and a stale generation cannot heartbeat, terminalize or
recover it.

The optional process-local rolling circuit may stop one worker sooner and emits into the
same global event path, but cannot enable, recover or replace PostgreSQL state. A worker
restart preserves the trip. `ADR065_APPLY_ENABLED` is only a static deployment admission
gate (default false); changing it never clears a runtime trip. UI polls the protected
readiness route at most every two seconds and disables immediately on any failed poll or
apply gate response. PPR reads and independently safe discovery never query this circuit.

Configuration is closed:
`ADR065_APPLY_ENABLED=false`,
`ADR065_STALE_RATE_WINDOW_SECONDS=900`,
`ADR065_STALE_RATE_MIN_REQUESTS=20`,
`ADR065_STALE_RATE_DISABLE_BPS=2500`,
`ADR065_ERROR_RATE_WINDOW_SECONDS=900`,
`ADR065_ERROR_RATE_MIN_REQUESTS=10`,
`ADR065_ERROR_RATE_DISABLE_BPS=1000`,
`ADR065_STOP_CONTROL_LOCK_TIMEOUT_MS=250`,
`ADR065_STOP_CONTROL_STATEMENT_TIMEOUT_MS=2000`,
`ADR065_STOP_CONTROL_COMMIT_ACK_TIMEOUT_MS=1000`,
`ADR065_ADMISSION_LEASE_SECONDS=5`,
`ADR065_ADMISSION_HEARTBEAT_INTERVAL_MS=1000`,
`ADR065_ADMISSION_SWEEP_INTERVAL_MS=1000`,
`ADR065_UI_READINESS_POLL_MS=2000`, and
`ADR065_UI_READINESS_HTTP_TIMEOUT_MS=3000`. Missing, malformed, negative or out-of-range
values use these stated fail-closed defaults and make apply not-ready; they never disable
the gate itself. At threshold equality the circuit trips. Expected 4xx completeness/
validation responses are excluded from the unexpected-error denominator; stale results
remain in the stale denominator.

The four latency contracts are separate. (1) Threshold detection has no polling: terminal
outcome recording starts synchronously, and healthy-DB evaluation is bounded by the 2000ms
control-statement timeout. (2) Detection and `TRIPPED` UPDATE are in that same transaction;
commit acknowledgement adds at most 1000ms, so healthy detection-to-ack is at most 1000ms
and invocation-to-ack at most `2000+1000=3000ms`. Lock wait is a subset capped at 250ms.
Timeout or unknown commit does not assert a latency success: it returns the stable
unavailable/commit-unknown reason, grants no admission, and requires authoritative reread;
an already durable admission remains visible and its `5s+1s+2s+1s=9s` worst-case
lease/sweep/evaluate/ack path globally trips after worker loss. (3) From a committed trip
to backend enforcement the authorization latency is zero cache intervals: every new apply
performs the uncached admission call and can never receive permission; healthy rejection
is returned within `2000+1000=3000ms`, while timeout also rejects. (4) UI state is advisory
and bounded separately by `2000+3000=5000ms` from committed trip; an apply click during
that display interval still meets the synchronous backend rejection. No 31-second claim
or transaction-wide guarantee is derived from PostgreSQL `statement_timeout`.

| Condition | Source and exact metric/event/readiness reason | Fail-closed condition and disabled component | Operator action and safe re-enable criterion |
|---|---|---|---|
| Writer drift | CI/startup manifest verifier; `adr065_writer_manifest_drift_total`, event `adr065.writer_manifest_drift`, reason `ADR065_WRITER_MANIFEST_DRIFT` | Any unknown writer, hash/SQL/route/catalog/proof mismatch: backend apply not-ready and UI apply disabled | Migrate/disable writer, regenerate from reviewed source, pass CI/startup/runtime/catalog proof, then explicitly re-enable |
| Watermark missing/stale/future | Same-transaction watermark read; `adr065_watermark_invalid_total{state}`, event `adr065.watermark_invalid`, reasons `ACTIVE_STATE_MISSING|ACTIVE_STATE_STALE|ACTIVE_STATE_FUTURE|ACTIVE_STATE_INVALID` | Any non-singleton or `effective_date != D`: preview classification blocked; apply/UI disabled | Restore authority writer, complete reviewed boundary run at D, prove singleton/current, rerun readiness |
| Boundary run incomplete/failed | `person_assignment_boundary_runs` readiness query; `adr065_boundary_run_not_ready_total{state}`, event `adr065.boundary_run_not_ready`, reason `ADR065_BOUNDARY_RUN_NOT_READY` | Any expired STARTED run, T1-incomplete evidence, unacknowledged prerequisite, failed current run or no valid current COMPLETED run: apply/UI disabled | Recover/finalize only through ADR-065 §11.3 functions, satisfy delivery/ACK/audit, prove current completed evidence and no expired STARTED run |
| Preview key ring | Startup/provider validation; `adr065_preview_keyring_failure_total`, event `adr065.preview_keyring_failure`, reason `ADR065_PREVIEW_KEY_RING_NOT_READY` | Any missing/malformed/ambiguous/unavailable ring: token-bearing preview, apply and UI apply disabled | Install valid dedicated ring, reload immutable snapshot, pass active/previous-key vectors and startup readiness |
| Operational IIN fingerprint key ring | Startup/provider validation; `adr065_iin_fingerprint_keyring_failure_total`, event `adr065.iin_fingerprint_keyring_failure`, reason `ADR065_IIN_FINGERPRINT_KEY_RING_NOT_READY` | Any missing/malformed/ambiguous/environment-mismatched ring: P0 confirmation/token, apply and UI apply disabled | Restore the dedicated profile ring, pass lifecycle/environment/separation vectors, rerun readiness |
| Idempotency IIN-binding key ring | Startup/provider validation; `adr065_idempotency_binding_keyring_failure_total`, event `adr065.idempotency_binding_keyring_failure`, reason `ADR065_IDEMPOTENCY_BINDING_KEY_RING_NOT_READY` | Any missing/malformed/ambiguous/unavailable required key: P0 confirmation/token, replay/apply and UI apply disabled | Restore dedicated retained binding keys, prove live operation/replay coverage and separation, rerun readiness |
| Expected-state protected-field key ring | Startup/provider validation; `adr065_expected_state_keyring_failure_total`, event `adr065.expected_state_keyring_failure`, reason `ADR065_EXPECTED_STATE_KEY_RING_NOT_READY` | Any missing/malformed/ambiguous/environment-mismatched ring: token-bearing preview, apply and UI apply disabled | Restore dedicated current/retained keys, reproduce protected-field/state digest vectors, rerun readiness |
| Evidence key ring | Evidence provider validation; `adr065_evidence_keyring_failure_total`, event `adr065.evidence_keyring_failure`, reason `ADR065_EVIDENCE_KEY_RING_NOT_READY` | Any required profile/key/secret unavailable or ambiguous: PERSONNEL_ORDER preview classification and all apply/UI enablement disabled | Restore approved ring/provider, verify profile/rotation fixtures without exposing secret, rerun readiness |
| Unique conflict | PostgreSQL exact constraint/index mapping; `adr065_unique_conflict_total{constraint}`, event `adr065.unique_conflict`, reason `ADR065_ASSIGNMENT_UNIQUE_CONFLICT` | First unexpected ADR assignment/application-key unique conflict: current transaction rolls back; process and global apply/UI circuit open immediately | Inventory conflicting state read-only, fix writer/protocol through reviewed change, pass catalog/concurrency gate; no SQL repair |
| Exclusion conflict | SQLSTATE 23P01 exact constraint; `adr065_exclusion_conflict_total`, event `adr065.exclusion_conflict`, reason `ADR065_ASSIGNMENT_EXCLUSION_CONFLICT` | First unexpected overlap conflict: rollback and immediate apply/UI stop | Correct writer/data cause through approved path, rerun overlap preflight/concurrency/catalog gate |
| Incomplete idempotency operation | Operation readiness query; `adr065_idempotency_incomplete_total`, event `adr065.idempotency_incomplete`, reason `IDEMPOTENCY_OPERATION_INCOMPLETE` | Any committed nonterminal/invalid operation row: apply/UI disabled; no blind retry | Resolve by reviewed idempotency recovery/forward fix, prove authoritative terminal/replay behavior, rerun gate |
| Audit failure | Strict audit port; `adr065_audit_failure_total`, event `adr065.audit_failure`, reason `ADR065_AUDIT_NOT_READY` | First audit write/validation failure: whole transaction rollback and immediate apply/UI stop | Restore audit writer/storage, pass fault-injection and persistence proof, rerun readiness |
| Provenance/binding failure | Import/provenance ports and final postcondition; `adr065_provenance_failure_total`, event `adr065.provenance_failure`, reason `ADR065_PROVENANCE_NOT_READY` | First unexpected provenance/import binding failure: whole rollback and immediate apply/UI stop | Correct authority/ownership or writer contract, pass exact-selection/FI proof and rerun gate |
| Unexpected stale rate | Apply result counter; `adr065_apply_stale_ratio_bps`, event `adr065.stale_rate_threshold`, reason `ADR065_STALE_RATE_EXCEEDED` | Rolling 900 s, at least 20 apply requests, stale outcomes >=2500 bps: apply/UI circuit open | Stop cohort, identify concurrency/preview-age cause, obtain one full clean window below threshold after fix, then explicit re-enable |
| Unexpected apply/error rate | Apply terminal counter; `adr065_apply_error_ratio_bps`, event `adr065.error_rate_threshold`, reason `ADR065_ERROR_RATE_EXCEEDED` | Rolling 900 s, at least 10 apply attempts, unexpected 5xx/DB/unmapped errors >=1000 bps: apply/UI circuit open | Stop cohort, correct cause, pass targeted regression and one full clean window below threshold, then explicit re-enable |

Every binary stop condition is atomically persisted through
`trip_adr065_runtime_stop`; rolling conditions are atomically evaluated by
`finalize_adr065_admission`, and `admit_adr065_apply` independently re-evaluates the same
committed window before granting admission. Thus all workers, not a process-local sample, determine
the two exact thresholds. Every stop disables new atomic apply plus the UI apply action
within the bound above. It does not alter PPR reads, compensate or reverse a
successfully committed transaction, or invoke automatic/manual SQL repair. Safe read-only
discovery may remain available only when its own watermark/schema/key and privacy gates
are satisfied; classification/token issuance obey their narrower blockers. Re-enablement
requires cause removal, `begin...`, a complete readiness rerun, and generation-CAS
`complete...`; a stale worker/readiness digest cannot re-enable.

**Multi-worker proofs.** PostgreSQL integration tests run at least two application worker
identities whose local counts remain below threshold but whose combined rows trip once.
They race admission and trip and assert one CAS transition; hold one long-running and one
`idle in transaction` business transaction and prove neither delays trip; inject control
lock/statement/commit-ack timeout; prove no admission succeeds after committed trip and a
pre-trip durable admission follows the stated full-commit/rollback rule; exercise lease
heartbeat, worker crash, one-second sweep and stale generation. They assert healthy
backend rejection <=3s and UI reflection <=5s, restart persistence, shared-store failure,
recovery only after new readiness digest, preservation of already committed results, and
that a local circuit can stop earlier but never globally enable or recover.

Migration/catalog tests assert exact table/CHECK/index/function definitions, immutable
transition evidence, fixed SECURITY-DEFINER search paths, runtime/recovery grants and
denial of direct DML. They separately prove admission insert/heartbeat/terminal CAS,
lease-expiry trip, runtime inability to mutate tables directly and no singleton/advisory
lock retained by the business transaction. Upgrade initializes exactly one ENABLED row;
downgrade is refused when state is TRIPPED/RECOVERING, any admission is nonterminal, or any
admission/event/transition evidence is retained. After any
runtime evidence exists the reviewed path is disable-and-forward-fix, never destructive
downgrade or manual cleanup.

### 11.4. Rollback

- Before any committed ADR-065 operation, disable endpoint/UI and downgrade only if the
  migration's catalog/data preflight permits it.
- After retained operation/event/assignment provenance exists, destructive downgrade is
  forbidden; disable apply and use a reviewed forward fix.
- Transaction failures require ordinary rollback, not compensation or manual cleanup.
- Rollback never deletes/relabels Employee events, orders, Persons, assignments, audit or
  provenance.
- The unchanged PPR resolver remains fail-closed throughout rollback.

---

## 12. Risks and implementation questions

| Risk/question | Disposition |
|---|---|
| Dirty untracked `k8` changes the locally visible Alembic head | Isolate ADR-065 work on a clean branch before creating the reserved migration chain; not an ADR blocker |
| Existing C2 helpers contain fallbacks/defaults | Extract strict caller-owned port from the same authority; do not create another writer |
| Existing reconciliation wrapper owns a transaction | Add exact `_tx` port and forbid the wrapper in the composite |
| Writer inventory is large and indirect | Closed W001–W594 matrix is policy; generated static/runtime/catalog manifest detects drift only |
| Legacy assignment data may violate new unique/exclusion rules | Report and stop; separate approved data remediation would be required, never implicit migration repair |
| Production evidence HMAC key/profile unavailable | Keep PERSONNEL_ORDER verification and apply fail-closed; EXTERNAL_REFERENCE does not waive other gates |
| P1 apply readiness trails P0 | Keep P1 server classification visible but write-disabled; no fallback to P0 or standalone modes |
| Reserved `m0→n1→o2` revision ID collides on the clean implementation branch | Stop before file creation and return the WP for revision metadata correction; do not choose a different chain silently |
| Readiness/feature configuration missing or malformed | Exact §11.3 fail-closed defaults apply; backend apply and UI apply remain disabled |

No open architecture decision is identified. The items above are bounded implementation
and rollout choices whose allowed behavior is already fixed by ADR-065. Discovery of a
required behavior outside that contract is a blocker and returns this WP to Architecture
Review rather than inventing a rule.

---

## 13. Definition of Done

This WP is done only when all of the following are true:

- implementation and normative baselines are recorded and no unreviewed ADR drift exists;
- preflight remains exact-IIN, read-only, safe and backend-authoritative;
- expected state and token cover the complete ADR finite state;
- preview wire forbids authoritative client state and the dedicated preview key ring passes
  startup, TTL and rotation gates without fallback;
- database operation/provenance/event/audit and assignment guarantees match ADR catalog
  definitions;
- boundary-run, delivery/ACK and onboarding retry schemas, roles, functions and C2
  lifecycle pass catalog, recovery, finalization and rollback proofs;
- strict ADR-048, C2 and reconciliation ports use one caller-owned transaction;
- composite P0 commits the complete chain or nothing;
- all FI statement ordinals, concurrency races, idempotency/retry and stale cases pass;
- events, strict audit and import provenance are complete and immutable;
- no one of the eight assignment decisions is inferred;
- PPR code is unchanged and the before/during/after behavior is proven;
- every W001–W594 disposition is implemented or technically disabled; writer manifest and
  startup/runtime/catalog rollout gates pass against the deployed artifact;
- all WP §11.3 immediate-stop signals and exact stale/error windows are proven to disable
  backend/UI apply without changing discovery or PPR reads;
- operator API is authorized, safe and replayable;
- UI blocks apply until explicit inputs and backend preview are complete;
- migrations and deployment have reviewed rollback/forward-fix evidence;
- no manual production SQL repair, unrelated refactor or standalone-mode semantic change
  is included;
- final backend and UI implementation reviews approve the exact diff.

---

## 14. Recommended first limited implementation slice

Implement **Slice 1 — Expected-state closure and preview token** only.

It is independently testable, adds no database write path or migration, preserves
`apply_available=false`, and converts the existing P0/P1 discovery into the exact trusted
input required by every later idempotency and atomicity step. Its closed review boundary
is the five existing production files and one existing API test named in Slice 1, the nine
new production modules and six new narrow test modules listed there. `app/main.py` and
`app/config.py` changes are limited to the four dedicated crypto providers and
startup/readiness wiring. It creates
no table, sequence, operation row, apply route, DML dispatcher or frontend code. Completion
requires the exact payload/digest/token/key-ring tests and production-path write-free proof
listed in Slice 1. Do not combine it with migrations, atomic apply or frontend work.

---

| IR065-001-R4 | **Closed, pending re-review** | §§9, 11.1–11.4, 13 | Short durable PostgreSQL admission serializes with trip but business transactions hold no stop lock; admitted work may finish atomically, new admission is synchronously denied, leases and four separate latency bounds are tested |

## 16. Completion and production validation

This WP's completion record is limited to the deployed Employee → Person repair flow. The
large forward-looking implementation decomposition above remains historical planning; this
closure does not represent an assertion that every broader enrollment or assignment item
was delivered.

### Delivered implementation

| Area | Commit / result |
|---|---|
| Person-link repair backend | `b51511f feat(personnel): implement ADR-065 person link repair flow` |
| Staff-card CTA and Person-card redirect | `95f50f6 fix(personnel): expose person link repair on staff card` |
| Approved-record eligibility | `1c3f501 fix(personnel): require approved records for ADR-065 repair` |
| Backend and isolated PostgreSQL suite | 2026-09-08: `147 passed, 2 skipped` on `corpsite_test`; create/link, replay, stale/fingerprint/payload hash, non-approved rejection, write-free refusal, authorization and server constraints. |
| Frontend suite | 2026-09-08: `5` files and `19 passed`; correct CTA visibility, explicit no-approved-record blocker, no apply when blocked, API wiring and Person-card redirect. |
| Deployment | Backend and frontend deployment of `1c3f501` completed successfully before production validation. |

### Production outcome

On 2026-09-08, the deployed commit `1c3f501` repaired Оразбеков Бактыбай
Сейхадирович: `employee_id=440` changed from `person_id=NULL` using approved
`normalized_record_id=4258` (batch `39`, `EDUCATION_GRADUATION`, `education_raw`) to a
linked Person `person_id=777`; control SQL returned `person_exists=true`. The working
card, its assignment and personnel-event history are available.

The negative Нурбеков check was fail-closed: its sole record was `superseded`, therefore
ADR-065 did not create a Person card. Neither case transfers education data. Correcting
Нурбеков's name through a supporting document and personnel order remains deferred work;
it is not an Excel correction. This record deliberately excludes a full IIN.

The R5 closure also contains a locally regression-proven cross-batch provenance
hardening. It is pending the ordinary backend deployment and does not alter the completed
positive production validation above.
